2026-10-18

In the Last 24 Hours

The AI industry continues to churn at a dizzying pace. Meta quietly revised its commercial terms of use after businesses pushed back against language that appeared to grant the company broad rights to scrape web content for AI training. Meanwhile, Anthropic published a candid internal report acknowledging that its workforce has grown more senior and that meetings have eaten into engineering time, a sign of how quickly these firms are maturing. On the competitive front, Replit CEO Amjad Masad announced at TechCrunch Disrupt that his AI coding platform has dropped Anthropic as its default model provider in favour of OpenAI, underscoring the fierce battle for developer-facing partnerships.

On the Apple side, the iPhone Duo is proving harder to get than expected, with employees instructed to warn walk-in customers that stock will be scarce on launch day and scalpers already inflating prices. For those who already own a fleet of Apple devices, a free indie app called Hoardware offers a tidy way to track models, serial numbers, and software versions across the entire collection, syncing through iCloud with no ads or in-app purchases.

Policy makers are also stepping into the digital goods conversation, with New York announcing a bill that would require video game publishers to grant players true ownership rather than revocable licences, following Pennsylvania's earlier move. And for developers hungry for practical knowledge, Hacker Noon has assembled a curated collection of 500 community blog posts documenting real-world projects built with GitHub, serving as a hands-on learning resource for anyone exploring those tools and workflows.

AI 業界嘅競爭依然激烈到令人眼花繚亂。Meta 喺企業界強烈反彈之後，悄悄修改咗商業條款，原本嘅條文睇落好似畀咗公司極大權利去抓取網頁內容嚟訓練 AI。Anthropic 就發咗一份內部報告，承認公司員工已經越嚟越資深化，開會時間亦明顯增加，反映呢啲公司成熟得好快。Replit 嘅 CEO Amjad Masad 喺 TechCrunch Disrupt 上面宣布，佢哋已經唔再用 Anthropic 做預設模型，改咗用 OpenAI，可見 AI 公司之間搶開發者平台合作夥伴搶到幾兇。

Apple 方面，iPhone Duo 開賣日預計會好難買到，員工已經被指示提醒即場買機嘅顧客，貨源可能只夠預購嘅人，炒家已經開始大幅加价。如果你手頭有一大堆 Apple 裝置，有一款免費獨立 app 叫 Hoardware，可以幫你追蹤所有裝置嘅型號、序號同軟件版本，透過 iCloud 同步，完全冇廣告亦冇內購。

政策層面都有新動向，紐約宣布一項法案，要求遊戲發行商必須畀玩家真正擁有已購買嘅遊戲，而唔係隨時可以撤回嘅授權，跟咗賓夕法尼亞州嘅腳步。另外，Hacker Noon 整理咗 500 篇社群部落格文章，全部係用 GitHub 做實戰專案嘅經驗分享，對於想學 GitHub 工具同工作流程嘅開發者嚟講，係一個好實用嘅學習資源。

Hacker News 20 articles

Last 24 Hours

HackerNews readers are buzzing about New York's new bill that would force video game publishers to grant players true ownership of their purchased titles instead of revocable licences. The legislation follows a similar move in Pennsylvania and reflects a growing political pushback against the industry's shift toward treating digital purchases as temporary rentals.

The project is MIT-licensed, requires no cloud or accounts, and represents a significant step toward making embedded development and testing accessible without physical hardware.

The Morris Worm Didn’t Break Anything. It Just Couldn’t Tell It Had Already Been There

https://hackernoon.com/the-morris-worm-didnt-break-anything-it-just-couldnt-tell-it-had-already-been-there📅 2026-10-16 19:03 UTC+8

The Morris Worm of 1988 caused widespread disruption not through destructive code but because it could not detect whether a system had already been infected, leading it to repeatedly reinfect the same machines. Every system it touched was a genuine target, and the cumulative effect of that relentless repetition brought large portions of the early internet to a standstill. The incident remains a cautionary tale about how even non-malicious automation can cause catastrophic harm when it lacks basic self-awareness.

1988年Morris Worm事件入面，條worm本身冇破壞性代碼，但係佢唔識得分辨部機已經中咗毒，所以就重複感染同一啲電腦，搞到成個早期互聯網癱瘓。佢行過每一道門都係真門，問題唔係出喺佢想搞破壞，而係佢冇能力知道自己已經去過。呢件事提醒我哋，就算自動化程式冇惡意，只要缺乏基本嘅自我辨識能力，都可以造成災難性嘅後果。

How to Use BBI Bollinger Bands (BBIBOLL) in U.S. Stock Trading: A Complete Beginner's Guide

https://hackernoon.com/how-to-use-bbi-bollinger-bands-bbiboll-in-us-stock-trading-a-complete-beginners-guide📅 2026-10-16 18:33 UTC+8

BBI Bollinger Bands (BBIBOLL) is a technical indicator that merges the Bull and Bear Index with Bollinger Bands, using the average of four simple moving averages (3, 6, 12, and 24-day) as its middle band to capture short- to medium-term price trends more responsively than traditional Bollinger Bands. The article outlines three core trading strategies — middle-band crossovers for trend direction, band squeezes for breakout identification, and outer-band touches for potential reversals — while emphasizing that no single indicator guarantees profits and should be confirmed with tools like RSI, MACD, and volume. It is particularly suited to swing and short-term traders who need faster signals on daily or intraday charts.

Rather than iterating slowly on single runs, he built a system using branch-driven isolation to fire off five parallel Spark experiments simultaneously, managed via an AI agent and persistent tmux sessions on cloud servers, which let him squeeze multiple experiment cycles into a single long-haul flight. The root cause turned out to be bloated per-event memory weight from UUID strings and Option boxing in a collect_list operator, and the fix of slimming individual events rather than adding cluster resources cut peak executor memory from 112% to 58% with zero disk spill.

The central principle is that an LLM judge must never operate alone and must be anchored in human judgment at every stage of its lifecycle, from benchmark creation through training, deployment, and ongoing drift monitoring. This matters because a quietly degrading judge can silently approve misleading or harmful outputs, eroding the very user trust the explanations are meant to build.

Netflix 嘅工程師詳細講咗佢哋點樣建立、校準同持續監察一個 LLM 裁判員，用嚟評估推薦解釋嘅

Yandex outage: Data shows that instead of going dark, malicious bots went on the move

https://blog.cloudflare.com/yandex-outage/📅 2026-10-15 04:24 UTC+8

Ukrainian drone strikes on Yandex data centers in early October knocked the company's networks offline, causing traffic to plummet by up to 99 percent, but analysis from Cloudflare shows that malicious bots did not go dark — they simply migrated to other Russian hosting providers like Radist and Selectel with identical fingerprints. The outage had very different effects on Yandex's two autonomous systems: the search ASN lost almost entirely legitimate search crawlers, while the cloud ASN's malicious automation, particularly targeting gambling sites, relocated seamlessly to other networks. The findings underscore that aggregate traffic drops can mislead observers into believing malicious operations have been disrupted when in reality the threat has merely shifted infrastructure.

Rewinding the exponential: How we keep hardware usage down

https://blog.cloudflare.com/rewinding-the-exponential/📅 2026-10-13 23:51 UTC+8

Cloudflare's Data team, which handles over two billion events per second across a network serving more than 20% of the web, has implemented a series of architectural changes over five years that collectively deferred roughly two and a half years of hardware expansion. Key moves include dismantling a monolithic data schema in 2022 to cut backbone bandwidth by 35-40%, switching from LZ4 to zstd compression in 2024 for another 40% throughput reduction, and in 2026 rearchitecting pipeline replication to eliminate double-writing across regions, yielding a net 36 GB/s reduction in wide-area transit traffic. These efforts matter because they demonstrate how engineering ingenuity can keep infrastructure costs in check against relentless exponential data growth.

Cloudflare 嘅 Data team 負責處理每秒超過二十億個事件，支撐住全球超過兩成嘅網絡流量，佢哋過去五年做咗一連串架構改動，等如推遲咗大約兩年半嘅硬件擴容。重點包括 2022 年拆散單一 schema 慳咗三四成頻寬、2024 年轉用 zstd 壓縮再減四成流量、以及 2026 年重新設計複製架構，將跨區傳輸淨減少咗 36 GB/s。呢啲做法證明咗靠工程智慧可以喺數據指數級增長之下控制到基礎設施成本。

This eliminates the need for a separate processing service by embedding a SQL transformation engine directly into Logpush's high-throughput export pipeline. The Agent Said It Was Done. The Database Disagreed.

https://huggingface.co/blog/microsoft/thinkingbox📅 2026-10-04 06:56 UTC+8

Microsoft's ThinkingBox benchmark evaluates AI agents by checking the actual database state they leave behind rather than trusting their self-reported success, revealing that 67% of failed attempts appeared clean on the surface. Across 507 business workflows tested on 18 models, the study exposed a dramatic gap between peak capability and reliability, with Kimi-K3 solving 93.89% of tasks at least once but only 13.41% consistently across all twenty attempts. The findings underscore that current AI agents remain far from dependable for real-world business operations where consistency matters more than occasional brilliance.

微軟嘅 ThinkingBox 測試唔再信 AI agent 自己講嘅嘢，而係直接查資料庫實際狀態，結果發現 67% 失敗嘅任務表面上睇落好似成功咁。研究用 18 個模型跑咗 507 個商業流程各二十次，發現巔峰表現同穩定性之間有巨大落差，Kimi-K3 至少有一次做到 93.89%，但二十次全部成功得 13.41%。呢個結果話畀我哋知，而家嘅 AI agent 離真正靠譜仲有好遠，企業唔好淨係睇佢講幾靚就信。

Don’t stop early: Case-folding source code at memory speed

https://github.blog/engineering/architecture-optimization/dont-stop-early-case-folding-source-code-at-memory-speed/📅 2026-08-01 00:00 UTC+8

GitHub engineers optimized case-folding for Blackbird, their code search engine that indexes over 480TB of source code, by discovering that removing all branches from the ASCII fast path—including the intuitive early-exit on non-ASCII bytes—allows the compiler to vectorize the loop and achieve over 45 GiB/s throughput, essentially memory bandwidth. The key technique replaces conditional logic with arithmetic: a bitwise OR accumulator detects non-ASCII bytes after the loop finishes, and a wrapping subtraction produces a branchless mask that folds uppercase letters by setting bit 5 unconditionally. The result, open-sourced as a Rust crate called casefold, demonstrates that at extreme scale, the branch itself is the enemy and counterintuitive choices like processing the entire buffer without stopping can be fifteen times faster than the obvious approach.

How Stripe uses graph search and state machines to auto-remediate a global database fleet

Understanding how this mechanism works at the level of logit distributions and sampling is key to evaluating whether watermarking benefits or harms the broader ecosystem of AI-generated text.

Controlling Reasoning Effort in LLMs

https://magazine.sebastianraschka.com/p/controlling-reasoning-effort-in-llms📅 2026-07-18 19:16 UTC+8

It has been almost two years since OpenAI released o1, a model that popularized the idea of LLM-based reasoning models. DeepSeek-R1 followed about four months later, together with details of a reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) recipe to train such reasoning models. Last week, OpenAI released the GPT-5.6 model family. It comes in three sizes, each with roughly five or six reasoning-effort settings. So yes, reasoning models are here to stay. They have become a standard part of modern model releases. In the past, I covered the methodology of reasoning models ( Understanding Reasoning LLMs ) as well as relevant research papers ( The State of Reinforcement Learning for LLM Reasoning and The State of LLM Reasoning Model Inference ). And I even wrote a whole new 440-page book on how to develop reasoning models, Build A Reasoning Model (From Scratch) . These resources have focused on turning a conventional LLM into a reasoning model. Now, in this article, I want to focus on and explain how to develop a reasoning model that has multiple effort modes, similar to what’s shown in the figure at the beginning of this article. No worries, this article can be read as a standalone article. However, the aforementioned resources may be interesting and useful. 1. A brief definition of reasoning models When talking about pretty much any machine learning or AI technique or subfield, the one lesson is that we usually shouldn’t take technical terms “literally”. For example, an (artificial) neural network in machine learning and AI doesn’t literally work like a biological neural network like the human brain. Similarly, when talking about “reasoning models”, we shouldn’t expect that these models literally reason like us humans. In the context of AI and LLM research, “reasoning model” means a model that outputs an intermediate reasoning trace, which is like an intermediate response that works through a question or task step by step. It’s probably easiest to explain this by showing an example. 2. A brief overview of training and inference scaling reasoning models There are essentially two ways to improve (reasoning) task performance: training scaling and inference scaling. Let’s briefly talk about training first. 2.1 Training reasoning models In a nutshell, DeepSeek-R1 proposed training an LLM using reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR) to turn it into a reasoning model. RLVR is a technique to provide a reward signal ( 0=incorrect and 1=correct ) for verifiable data domains. These verifiable data domains here are math (we can use a symbolic math checker like SymPy or WolframAlpha to check results) and code (we can use a compiler or unit tests, or integrated platforms like LeetCode) to check for correctness. Notably, the reasoning trace itself was not used for training or updating the model. Although they tried to use this intermediate response information for training, the DeepSeek-R1 paper reported that it wasn’t helpful for the model training, so it was ultimately not used. (Whether and how to incorporate intermediate reasoning traces in the training signal via process reward models is an active area of research.) 2.2 “Aha” moments Anyway, just training on the output rewards alone, as Figure 7 shows, turned out to be sufficient for the model to learn how to reason through a problem, meaning that it would learn to write intermediate explanations, backtrack, and self-correct itself. These moments when the model realizes that it made a mistake and self-corrects itself are called “Aha” moments. By the way, while DeepSeek-R1 is inarguably the more popular paper, and the paper that created excitement around reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards and the development of reasoning models, there is another paper, Kimi K1.5 , published on exactly the same day on arXiv (22 Jan 2025). Also, the term RLVR was already coined two months earlier in Tülu 3: Pushing Frontiers in Open Language Model Post-Training . ​ One reason why the DeepSeek R1 is ultimately the more popular paper is that it demonstrated that reasoning behavior can be achieved with pure reinforcement learning (RL). For instance, Tülu 3 and Kimi K1.5 applied reinforcement learning on top of a supervised fine-tuned (SFT) model. The DeepSeek-R1 model was also trained from an SFT checkpoint of the DeepSeek-V3 base model, and it included a DeepSeek-R1-Zero variant trained with pure RLVR. R1 Zero is a weaker model than R1, but it showed that RLVR is sufficient for teaching the model to generate and use reasoning traces. ​While R1-Zero was more of a proof-of-concept model, note that the full DeepSeek-R1 reasoning model training pipeline is usually multi-stage and a bit more complicated, as mentioned above. ​ By the way, most of today’s LLMs are effectively reasoning models, meaning they have been trained in a similar fashion to DeepSeek-R1 using a form of RLVR. 2.3 Inference scaling in a nutshell Next to improving reasoning behavior through training, another lever for improving model performance is inference compute scaling. In short, this means that we are spending more compute after training the model, during usage, to get better answers. This is a whole topic by itself, and you could read through my The State of LLM Reasoning Model Inference for a more detailed rundown: I will try to summarize what’s most essential to mention as background info below. First, training a model with RLVR is already implicitly leading to a form of inference scaling, since reasoning models usually output more tokens during inference compared to conventional LLMs, and that means we are spending more compute during inference. Second, we can further adjust this output length via reasoning effort levels, but more on that later. ​Third, there are many additional inference scaling techniques. A popular one is self-consistency, which is often implemented as a form of majority voting where the model is queried multiple times, and the final answer is selected via majority vote. This can be applied to conventional LLMs as well as reasoning models. Also, this method can be used on demand and in addition to reasoning training. A good example of that is DeepSeekMath-V2, where the researchers applied extreme inference-scaling on top of a reasoning model (specialized for math) to achieve state-of-the-art performance on challenging math olympiad-type problems. But again, I will refer to my other article, The State of LLM Reasoning Model Inference for an overview of other techniques: 3. Think tokens You may have seen the <think></think> tokens in the earlier “Aha moments” figure. I also included the corresponding figure below so you don’t have to scroll all the way up. ​ These <think> and </think> tags are cosmetic with respect to reasoning ability. They do not make the model reason, and they are not required to achieve good reasoning performance. One could train the same model without these delimiters and likely reach similar benchmark performance. The purpose of these <think> tags or tokens is mainly to mark where the reasoning trace begins and ends so that the training pipeline or user interface can separate it from the final answer and optionally hide it from the user. (UIs like ChatGPT or Codex usually do this.) The point here is that the <think> tokens are not giving the model the ability to “think” or reason or reason better. One could train the same models without such <think> tokens and reach similar benchmark performance. There is also nothing special about the literal strings <think> and </think> . Another pair of delimiters could serve the same purpose. By the way, the way this is implemented is typically by adding a formatting reward during the RLVR stage. So instead of just rewarding the model based on answer correctness, one would provide additional reward for the use of <think> tokens, which in turn encourages the model to use those. In DeepSeek-R1, for example, the overall reward was calculated as R_total

Read full article →
